Use vectorized operations instead of loops for better performance\n4. Use `str.contains()` with `na=False` to avoid errors with missing values\n5. df.groupby('column').agg({\n    'value_col': 'sum',\n    'count_col': 'count'\n})\n```\n\n### Sorting\n```python\n# Sort by column (ascending)\nsorted_df = df.sort_values('column')\n\n# Sort by column df.nlargest(5, 'value_column')\n```\n\n### Merging Data\n```python\n# Inner join\nmerged = pd.merge(df1, df2, on='key_column', how='inner')\n\n# Left join\nmerged = pd.merge(df1, df2, on='key_column', It discloses their equity holdings.\n\n## Data Structure\n\n### Key Files\nThe SEC Form 13F dataset consists of multiple TSV files:\n\n1. **COVERPAGE.tsv** - Basic information about the filing and filing manager\n2. **SUBMISSION.tsv** - Filing metadata (dates, CIK, submission type)\n3. **SUMMARYPAGE.tsv** - Summary statistics (total value, number of entries)\n4. **INFOTABLE.tsv** - Detailed holdings information (securities held)\n5. **OTHERMANAGER.tsv** - Information about other managers\n6. **SIGNATURE.tsv** - Signatory information\n\n### Primary Key\n- **ACCESSION_NUMBER** - Unique identifier for each filing (used across all tables)\n\n### Important Fields\n\n#### COVERPAGE.tsv\n- `ACCESSION_NUMBER` - Unique filing identifier\n- `FILINGMANAGER_NAME` - Name of the fund/manager\n- `REPORTCALENDARORQUARTER` - Reporting period end date\n- `REPORTTYPE` - Type of report (Holdings, Notice, Combination)\n\n#### SUBMISSION.tsv\n- `ACCESSION_NUMBER` - Links to other tables\n- `CIK` - Central Index Key (unique identifier for the entity)\n- `FILING_DATE` - When the report was filed\n- `PERIODOFREPORT` - The period covered by the report\n\n#### SUMMARYPAGE.tsv\n- `ACCESSION_NUMBER` - Links to other tables\n- `TABLEENTRYTOTAL` - Number of different securities held\n- `TABLEVALUETOTAL` - Total value of all holdings (AUM in dollars)\n\n#### INFOTABLE.tsv\n- `ACCESSION_NUMBER` - Links to the specific filing\n- `NAMEOFISSUER` - Company name\n- `CUSIP` - Unique identifier for the security\n- `VALUE` - Market value in dollars (rounded to nearest dollar since Jan 2023)\n- `SSHPRNAMT` - Number of shares or principal amount\n- `SSHPRNAMTTYPE` - Type: \"SH\" for shares, \"PRN\" for principal\n\n## Common Analysis Workflows\n\n### 1. Find Fund by Name (Fuzzy Search)\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# Read coverpage\ncoverpage = pd.read_csv('COVERPAGE.tsv', sep='\\t', low_memory=False)\n\n# Fuzzy search for fund name (case-insensitive)\nsearch_term = 'renaissance technologies'\nmask = coverpage['FILINGMANAGER_NAME'].str.lower().str.contains(search_term, na=False)\nmatches = coverpage[mask]\n\n# Get the accession number\naccession_number = matches.iloc[0]['ACCESSION_NUMBER']\n```\n\n### 2. Get Fund AUM (Assets Under Management)\n```python\n# Read summary page\nsummary = pd.read_csv('SUMMARYPAGE.tsv', sep='\\t', low_memory=False)\n\n# Get AUM for specific fund\nfund_summary = summary[summary['ACCESSION_NUMBER'] == accession_number]\naum = fund_summary.iloc[0]['TABLEVALUETOTAL']\n```\n\n### 3. Count Holdings for a Fund\n```python\n# Option 1: From summary page\nnum_holdings = fund_summary.iloc[0]['TABLEENTRYTOTAL']\n\n# Option 2: From infotable (more accurate if data is available)\ninfotable = pd.read_csv('INFOTABLE.tsv', sep='\\t', low_memory=False)\nholdings = infotable[infotable['ACCESSION_NUMBER'] == accession_number]\nnum_holdings = len(holdings)\n```\n\n### 4. Compare Holdings Between Quarters\n```python\n# Load holdings for Q2\ninfotable_q2 = pd.read_csv('2025-q2/INFOTABLE.tsv', sep='\\t', low_memory=False)\nholdings_q2 = infotable_q2[infotable_q2['ACCESSION_NUMBER'] == accession_q2]\n\n# Load holdings for Q3\ninfotable_q3 = pd.read_csv('2025-q3/INFOTABLE.tsv', sep='\\t', low_memory=False)\nholdings_q3 = infotable_q3[infotable_q3['ACCESSION_NUMBER'] == accession_q3]\n\n# Merge on CUSIP to compare\nmerged = pd.merge(\n    holdings_q2[['CUSIP', 'VALUE', 'NAMEOFISSUER']],\n    holdings_q3[['CUSIP', 'VALUE']],\n    on='CUSIP',\n    how='outer',\n    suffixes=('_q2', '_q3')\n)\n\n# Fill missing values with 0 (stock not held in that quarter)\nmerged['VALUE_q2'] = merged['VALUE_q2'].fillna(0)\nmerged['VALUE_q3'] = merged['VALUE_q3'].fillna(0)\n\n# Calculate change\nmerged['change'] = merged['VALUE_q3'] - merged['VALUE_q2']\n\n# Get top increases\ntop_increases = merged.nlargest(5, 'change')\n```\n\n### 5. Find CUSIP by Company Name\n```python\n# Search infotable for company name\nsearch_term = 'palantir'\nmask = infotable['NAMEOFISSUER'].str.lower().str.contains(search_term, na=False)\nmatches = infotable[mask]\n\n# Get unique CUSIP\ncusip = matches.iloc[0]['CUSIP']\n```\n\n## Important Notes\n\n1. **Accession numbers change between quarters** - The same fund will have different accession numbers for different reporting periods\n2. **VALUE is in dollars** - Starting January 2023, values are rounded to nearest dollar (not thousands)\n3. **Case-insensitive search** - Always use `.str.lower()` when searching names\n4. **Missing data** - Some funds may not appear in both quarters (new funds, funds below threshold, etc.)\n5. **Multiple filings** - Some large funds file multiple 13Fs (check for amendments)\n\n## Performance Tips\n\n1. Case-Insensitive Substring Search (Pandas)\nThe simplest approach for basic fuzzy matching:\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\ndf = pd.read_csv('data.tsv', sep='\\t')\n\n# Search for substring (case-insensitive)\nsearch_term = 'renaissance technologies'\nmask = df['name_column'].str.lower().str.contains(search_term.lower(), na=False)\nmatches = df[mask]\n```\n\n### 2. Advanced Libraries\n\n#### fuzzywuzzy / rapidfuzz\nFor more sophisticated fuzzy matching with similarity scores:\n\n```bash\npip install rapidfuzz\n```\n\n```python\nfrom rapidfuzz import fuzz, process\n\n# Simple ratio (0-100 similarity score)\nscore = fuzz.ratio('renaissance technologies', 'Renaissance Technologies LLC')\n# Returns: ~85\n\n# Partial ratio (finds best matching substring)\nscore = fuzz.partial_ratio('renaissance', 'Renaissance Technologies LLC')\n# Returns: 100\n\n# Token sort ratio (ignores word order)\nscore = fuzz.token_sort_ratio('technologies renaissance', 'Renaissance Technologies')\n# Returns: 100\n\n# Find best match from a list\nchoices = ['Renaissance Technologies LLC', 'Berkshire Hathaway', 'BlackRock']\nbest_match = process.extractOne('renaissance tech', choices)\n# Returns: ('Renaissance Technologies LLC', score, index)\n\n# Find top N matches\ntop_matches = process.extract('renaissance', choices, limit=3)\n```\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n### Find Best Match in DataFrame\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom rapidfuzz import fuzz, process\n\ndf = pd.read_csv('data.tsv', sep='\\t')\nsearch_term = 'renaissance technologies'\n\n# Method 1: Using str.contains (fast, good for simple cases)\nmask = df['name'].str.lower().str.contains(search_term.lower(), na=False)\nsimple_matches = df[mask]\n\n# Method 2: Using rapidfuzz (more accurate)\nnames = df['name'].tolist()\nbest_match = process.extractOne(search_term, names, scorer=fuzz.token_sort_ratio)\nif best_match and best_match[1] > 80:  # 80% similarity threshold\n    best_name = best_match[0]\n    result = df[df['name'] == best_name]\n```\n\n### Batch Fuzzy Matching\n```python\ndef find_best_match(search_term, df, column_name, threshold=80):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find the best fuzzy match in a dataframe column.\n\n    Args:\n        search_term: String to search for\n        df: DataFrame to search in\n        column_name: Column to search\n        threshold: Minimum similarity score (0-100)\n\n    Returns:\n        DataFrame row with best match or None\n    \"\"\"\n    from rapidfuzz import process, fuzz\n\n    # Get all values from column\n    values = df[column_name].dropna().tolist()\n\n    # Find best match\n    result = process.extractOne(\n        search_term,\n        values,\n        scorer=fuzz.token_sort_ratio\n    )\n\n    if result and result[1] >= threshold:\n        best_value = result[0]\n        return df[df[column_name] == best_value]\n\n    return None\n```\n\n## Scoring Methods Comparison\n\n### ratio()\n- Compares entire strings\n- Order matters\n- Best for: Exact or near-exact matches\n\n### partial_ratio()\n- Finds best matching substring\n- Good for: Finding short string within longer string\n- Example: \"tech\" in \"Renaissance Technologies\"\n\n### token_sort_ratio()\n- Ignores word order\n- Tokenizes and sorts words\n- Best for: Names with different word orders\n- Example: \"John Smith\" vs \"Smith, John\"\n\n### token_set_ratio()\n- Similar to token_sort_ratio but handles duplicates better\n- Best for: Names with extra words\n- Example: \"Renaissance Technologies\" vs \"Renaissance Technologies LLC\"\n\n## When to Use Each Method\n\n### Use str.contains() when:\n- You need fast performance on large datasets\n- Search term is a clear substring\n- Don't need similarity scoring\n- Case-insensitive search is sufficient\n\n### Use fuzzywuzzy/rapidfuzz when:\n- Need to handle typos or variations\n- Need similarity scoring\n- Want to find \"closest match\"\n- Dealing with user input or inconsistent data\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Normalize first**: Convert to lowercase, remove extra spaces\n```python\ndf['name_clean'] = df['name'].str.lower().str.strip()\n```\n\n2. **Remove common suffixes**: LLC, Inc, Corp, etc. for better matching\n```python\ndef clean_company_name(name):\n    suffixes = ['llc', 'inc', 'corp', 'corporation', 'limited', 'ltd']\n    name_lower = name.lower()\n    for suffix in suffixes:\n        name_lower = name_lower.replace(suffix, '').strip()\n    return name_lower\n```\n\n3. **Use appropriate threshold**:\n   - 90-100: Very strict (typos only)\n   - 80-90: Moderate (variations allowed)\n   - 70-80: Loose (significant differences allowed)\n\n4. **Combine methods**:\n```python\n# First try exact substring match (fast)\nmask = df['name'].str.lower().str.contains(search_term.lower(), na=False)\nif mask.sum() > 0:\n    return df[mask]\nelse:\n    # Fall back to fuzzy matching\n    return find_best_match(search_term, df, 'name')\n```\n\n## Performance Considerations\n\n- `str.contains()` is much faster than fuzzy matching\n- Use fuzzy matching only when necessary\n- For large datasets, filter first with simple methods, then apply fuzzy matching\n- `rapidfuzz` is faster than `fuzzywuzzy` (C++ implementation)\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":3,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1984,"cache_read_input_tokens":30909,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1984,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8102ac58-52de-487a-8e94-7f9a8f0e2e5c","uuid":"1a550196-3079-4f79-b623-70a5a7c470c2"}

'renaissance technologies'\n\n# Method 1: Using str.contains (fast, good for simple cases)\nmask = df['name'].str.lower().str.contains(search_term.lower(), na=False)\nsimple_matches = df[mask]\n\n# Method 2: Using rapidfuzz (more accurate)\nnames = df['name'].tolist()\nbest_match = process.extractOne(search_term, names, scorer=fuzz.token_sort_ratio)\nif best_match and best_match[1] > 80:  # 80% similarity threshold\n    best_name = best_match[0]\n    result = df[df['name'] == best_name]\n```\n\n### Batch Fuzzy Matching\n```python\ndef find_best_match(search_term, df, column_name, threshold=80):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find the best fuzzy match in a dataframe column.\n\n    Args:\n        search_term: String to search for\n        df: DataFrame to search in\n        column_name: Column to search\n        threshold: Minimum similarity score (0-100)\n\n    Returns:\n        DataFrame row with best match or None\n    \"\"\"\n    from rapidfuzz import process, fuzz\n\n    # Get all values from column\n    values = df[column_name].dropna().tolist()\n\n    # Find best match\n    result = process.extractOne(\n        search_term,\n        values,\n        scorer=fuzz.token_sort_ratio\n    )\n\n    if result and result[1] >= threshold:\n        best_value = result[0]\n        return df[df[column_name] == best_value]\n\n    return None\n```\n\n## Scoring Methods Comparison\n\n### ratio()\n- Compares entire strings\n- Order matters\n- Best for: Exact or near-exact matches\n\n### partial_ratio()\n- Finds best matching substring\n- Good for: Finding short string within longer string\n- Example: \"tech\" in \"Renaissance Technologies\"\n\n### token_sort_ratio()\n- Ignores word order\n- Tokenizes and sorts words\n- Best for: Names with different word orders\n- Example: \"John Smith\" vs \"Smith, John\"\n\n### token_set_ratio()\n- Similar to token_sort_ratio but handles duplicates better\n- Best for: Names with extra words\n- Example: \"Renaissance Technologies\" vs \"Renaissance Technologies LLC\"\n\n## When to Use Each Method\n\n### Use str.contains() when:\n- You need fast performance on large datasets\n- Search term is a clear substring\n- Don't need similarity scoring\n- Case-insensitive search is sufficient\n\n### Use fuzzywuzzy/rapidfuzz when:\n- Need to handle typos or variations\n- Need similarity scoring\n- Want to find \"closest match\"\n- Dealing with user input or inconsistent data\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Normalize first**: Convert to lowercase, remove extra spaces\n```python\ndf['name_clean'] = df['name'].str.lower().str.strip()\n```\n\n2. **Remove common suffixes**: LLC, Inc, Corp, etc. for better matching\n```python\ndef clean_company_name(name):\n    suffixes = ['llc', 'inc', 'corp', 'corporation', 'limited', 'ltd']\n    name_lower = name.lower()\n    for suffix in suffixes:\n        name_lower = name_lower.replace(suffix, '').strip()\n    return name_lower\n```\n\n3. **Use appropriate threshold**:\n   - 90-100: Very strict (typos only)\n   - 80-90: Moderate (variations allowed)\n   - 70-80: Loose (significant differences allowed)\n\n4. **Combine methods**:\n```python\n# First try exact substring match (fast)\nmask = df['name'].str.lower().str.contains(search_term.lower(), na=False)\nif mask.sum() > 0:\n    return df[mask]\nelse:\n    # Fall back to fuzzy matching\n    return find_best_match(search_term, df, 'name')\n```\n\n## Performance Considerations\n\n- `str.contains()` is much faster than fuzzy matching\n- Use fuzzy matching only when necessary\n- For large datasets, filter first with simple methods, then apply fuzzy matching\n- `rapidfuzz` is faster than `fuzzywuzzy` (C++ implementation)\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

 \"name\": \"John Doe\",\n    \"age\": 30,\n    \"city\": \"New York\"\n}\n\nwith open('output.json', 'w') as f:\n    json.dump(data, f)\n```\n\n#### Pretty Printing (Indented)\n```python\nwith open('output.json', 'w') as f:\n    json.dump(data, f, indent=4)\n```\n\n#### Convert to String\n```python\njson_string = json.dumps(data, indent=4)\nprint(json_string)\n```\n\n### Reading JSON from indent='\\t')\n```\n\n### Sorting Keys\n```python\ndata = {\"z\": 1, \"a\": 2, \"m\": 3}\n\n# Sort keys alphabetically\njson.dump(data, f, sort_keys=True, indent=4)\n# Output: {\"a\": 2, \"m\": 3, pd.merge(\n        holdings_q2,\n        holdings_q3,\n        on='CUSIP',\n        how='outer',\n        suffixes=('_q2', '_q3')\n    )\n\n    # Fill missing values with 0 (stock not held in that quarter)\n    merged['VALUE_q2'] = merged['VALUE_q2'].fillna(0)\n    merged['VALUE_q3'] = merged['VALUE_q3'].fillna(0)\n\n    # Calculate change\n    merged['change'] = merged['VALUE_q3'] - merged['VALUE_q2']\n\n    # Get top increases (must be positive increase)\n    increases = merged[merged['change'] > 0].copy()\n    top_increases = increases.nlargest(top_n, 'change')\n\n    print(f\"\\n  Top {top_n} increased investments:\")\n    for idx, row in top_increases.iterrows():\n        name = row['NAMEOFISSUER'] if pd.notna(row['NAMEOFISSUER']) else 'N/A'\n        print(f\"    {row['CUSIP']}: {name}\")\n        print(f\"      Q2: ${row['VALUE_q2']:,.0f} -> Q3: ${row['VALUE_q3']:,.0f}\")\n        print(f\"      Increase: ${row['change']:,.0f}\")\n\n    cusips = Accession number of the best match\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"Searching for fund: {fund_name}\")\n\n    coverpage = pd.read_csv(coverpage_path, sep='\\t', low_memory=False)\n\n    # Fuzzy search (case-insensitive substring match)\n    mask = coverpage['FILINGMANAGER_NAME'].str.lower().str.contains(\n        fund_name.lower(), na=False\n    )\n    matches = coverpage[mask]\n\n    if len(matches) == 0:\n        raise ValueError(f\"No matches found for '{fund_name}'\")\n\n    # If prefer_holdings_report, filter to only holdings reports\n    if prefer_holdings_report:\n        holdings_matches = matches[matches['REPORTTYPE'].str.contains('HOLDINGS', na=False)]\n        if len(holdings_matches) > 0:\n            matches = holdings_matches\n\n    # Take the first match\n    accession = matches.iloc[0]['ACCESSION_NUMBER']\n    matched_name = matches.iloc[0]['FILINGMANAGER_NAME']\n\n    print(f\"  Found: {matched_name}\")\n    print(f\"  Accession: {accession}\")\n\n    return accession\n\n\ndef get_fund_aum(summarypage_path, accession_number):\n    \"\"\"\n    Get Assets Under Management (AUM) for a fund.\n\n    Args:\n        summarypage_path: Path to SUMMARYPAGE.tsv file\n        accession_number: Accession number of the fund\n\n    Returns:\n        Total value (AUM) as integer\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"Getting AUM for accession: {accession_number}\")\n\n    summary = pd.read_csv(summarypage_path, sep='\\t', low_memory=False)\n    fund_summary = summary[summary['ACCESSION_NUMBER'] == accession_number]\n\n    if len(fund_summary) == 0:\n        raise ValueError(f\"No summary found for accession {accession_number}\")\n\n    aum = int(fund_summary.iloc[0]['TABLEVALUETOTAL'])\n    print(f\"  AUM: ${aum:,}\")\n\n    return aum\n\n\ndef count_fund_holdings(summarypage_path, accession_number):\n    \"\"\"\n    Count number of stocks held by a fund.\n\n    Args:\n        summarypage_path: Path to SUMMARYPAGE.tsv get_top_increased_investments(infotable_q2_path, infotable_q3_path,\n                                   accession_q2, accession_q3, top_n=5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find top N stocks with increased investment between Q2 and Q3.\n\n    Args:\n        infotable_q2_path: Path to Q2 INFOTABLE.tsv\n        infotable_q3_path: Path to Q3 INFOTABLE.tsv\n        accession_q2: Accession number for Q2\n        accession_q3: Accession number for Q3\n        top_n: Number of top stocks to return\n\n    Returns:\n        List of CUSIPs with highest dollar value increases\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"Comparing infotable_q2 = pd.read_csv(infotable_q2_path, sep='\\t', low_memory=False)\n    holdings_q2 = infotable_q2[infotable_q2['ACCESSION_NUMBER'] == accession_q2]\n    holdings_q2 = holdings_q2[['CUSIP', 'VALUE', 'NAMEOFISSUER']].copy()\n\n    # Read Q3 holdings\n    print(\"  Loading Q3 holdings...\")\n    infotable_q3 = pd.read_csv(infotable_q3_path, sep='\\t', low_memory=False)\n    holdings_q3 = infotable_q3[infotable_q3['ACCESSION_NUMBER'] == accession_q3]\n    holdings_q3 = holdings_q3[['CUSIP', 'VALUE']].copy()\n\n    print(f\"  Q2 holdings: {len(holdings_q2)} stocks\")\n    print(f\"  Q3 holdings: {len(holdings_q3)} stocks\")\n\n    # Merge on CUSIP\n    merged = pd.merge(\n        holdings_q2,\n        holdings_q3,\n        on='CUSIP',\n        how='outer',\n        suffixes=('_q2', '_q3')\n    )\n\n    # Fill missing values with 0 (stock not held in that quarter)\n    merged['VALUE_q2'] = merged['VALUE_q2'].fillna(0)\n    merged['VALUE_q3'] = merged['VALUE_q3'].fillna(0)\n\n    # Calculate change\n    merged['change'] = merged['VALUE_q3'] - merged['VALUE_q2']\n\n    # Get top increases (must be positive increase)\n    increases = merged[merged['change'] > 0].copy()\n    top_increases = increases.nlargest(top_n, 'change')\n\n    print(f\"\\n  Top {top_n} increased investments:\")\n    for idx, row in top_increases.iterrows():\n        name = row['NAMEOFISSUER'] if quarter)\n    merged['VALUE_q2'] = merged['VALUE_q2'].fillna(0)\n    merged['VALUE_q3'] = merged['VALUE_q3'].fillna(0)\n\n    # Combine names (prefer Q3, fallback to Q2)\n    merged['NAMEOFISSUER'] = positive increase)\n    increases = merged[merged['change'] > 0].copy()\n    top_increases = increases.nlargest(top_n, 'change')\n\n    print(f\"\\n  Top {top_n} increased investments:\")\n    for idx, row in top_increases.iterrows():\n        name = row['NAMEOFISSUER'] if pd.notna(row['NAMEOFISSUER']) else 'N/A'\n        print(f\"    {row['CUSIP']}: {name}\")\n        print(f\"      Q2: ${row['VALUE_q2']:,.0f} -> Q3: ${row['VALUE_q3']:,.0f}\")\n        print(f\"      Increase: ${row['change']:,.0f}\")\n\n    cusips = top_increases['CUSIP'].tolist()\n    return cusips\n\n\ndef find_cusip_by_name(infotable_path, company_name):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find CUSIP for a company by searching its name.\n\n    Args:\n        infotable_path: Path to INFOTABLE.tsv file\n matches = infotable[mask]\n\n    if len(matches) == 0:\n        raise ValueError(f\"No matches found for '{company_name}'\")\n\n    # Get unique CUSIPs\n    unique_cusips = matches['CUSIP'].unique()\n    cusip = unique_cusips[0]\n\n    company_full_name = matches[matches['CUSIP'] == cusip].iloc[0]['NAMEOFISSUER']\n    print(f\"  Found: {company_full_name}\")\n    print(f\"  CUSIP: {cusip}\")\n\n    return cusip\n\n\ndef get_top_holders(infotable_path, coverpage_path, cusip, top_n=3):\n    \"\"\"\n    Find top N fund managers holding a specific stock.\n\n    Args:\n        infotable_path: Path to = pd.merge(\n        stock_holdings,\n        coverpage[['ACCESSION_NUMBER', 'FILINGMANAGER_NAME']],\n        on='ACCESSION_NUMBER',\n        how='left'\n    )\n\n    # Sort by value and get top holders\n    top_holders = holdings_with_names.nlargest(top_n, 'VALUE')\n\n    print(f\"\\n  Top {top_n} holders:\")\n    for idx, row in top_holders.iterrows():\n        print(f\"    {row['FILINGMANAGER_NAME']}\")\n        print(f\"      Value: ${row['VALUE']:,.0f}\")\n\n    fund_names = top_holders['FILINGMANAGER_NAME'].tolist()\n    return fund_names\n\n\ndef main():\n
